# Image Toolkit — Changelog

*Completed items archived from the Master Roadmap. Ordered from most recent phase to earliest.*

## S362 — 2026-08-07 (Portable character style-bible foundation)

Started reference-consistent coloring Phase 2 while keeping tutor #10/#11/#12
frozen in Review. C1 #15 delivered a standalone versioned semantic material and
cel-palette contract with atomic bounded recovery (`e79776c`). C2 #16 bound
validated bibles/reference views into portable schema-v9 projects with safe
relative non-symlink assets, attach/detach engine operations, migration, and
pixel-free summaries (`79ee7d6`). Verification: 325 tests, Ruff and core mypy
clean. Strict MkDocs could not start because the optional `mkdocstrings` plugin
is absent in the current environment; dependencies were not changed implicitly.

## S361 — 2026-08-07 (A3 moved to deferred review)

dispatch, form/cast masks, decisions and rationale revisions, cancellation,
dashboard behavior, offline use, and artist-layer isolation. All-pass moves the
issue to Done; any failure documents the step and returns it to In Progress.
The candidate remains undeployed. #10/#11 also remain in Review; A4 #14 stays
in Backlog.

## S360 — 2026-08-07 (Sequential fresh capstone review dispatch)

Completed Cel-Shaded-Generator A3's headless fresh capstone dispatcher
(`6640b85`). “Run Next Capstone Review” now opens the correct rubric-specific
landmark flow immediately and advances after a persisted decision/rationale.
Multi-rubric layers run sequentially in structural-leverage order rather than
sharing incompatible landmark sets. Dedicated capstone front/turned form/cast
and optional-third-value layers support the lighting stage. Cancellation leaves
recoverable pending state. Verification: 309 tests, Ruff and core mypy clean.
A3 remains undeployed pending live confirmation; #10/#11 stay in Review.

## S333 — 2026-08-07 (Krita 5.x review redline layers)

Advanced Cel-Shaded-Generator A2 issue #10 (`e41af94`) with Krita 5.x-compatible redline rendering. The adapter avoids Krita 6-only painting methods: it validates and bounds normalized engine geometry, rasterizes transparent U8 BGRA corrections, and writes only to a new locked tutor-owned layer inside the always-relocked feedback group; a failed write removes its partial layer. Artist layers are never selected or modified. Verification: 184 tests, Ruff and mypy clean. With exact process-name confirmation that Krita was closed, the updated plugin and configured engine were safely redeployed. Live redline verification remains.

## S332 — 2026-08-07 (End-to-end landmark review explanations)

Advanced Cel-Shaded-Generator A2 issue #10 (`22f629d`) through the first end-to-end review path. The installer atomically records an explicitly selected engine executable in XDG configuration; the dependency-free plugin validates/discovers it, submits the nine normalized landmarks under a unique request identifier, and displays deterministic explanations while leaving artwork unchanged. Missing landmarks, engine/configuration failures, and incomplete results are actionable UI states. Verification: 179 tests, Ruff and mypy clean. With Krita confirmed closed by exact process name, the plugin and engine configuration were deployed and a real configured-engine review smoke test passed. Redline layer rendering remains.

## S331 — 2026-08-07 (Krita manual landmark editor)

Advanced Cel-Shaded-Generator A2 issue #10 (`d5729f5`) with a manual nine-point landmark editor over a public document-projection snapshot. Because Krita's public Python Canvas API exposes no screen-to-image conversion, the adapter avoids private Qt canvas internals; it labels ordered markers, supports undo/reset/cancel, normalizes coordinates, derives cranial radius, and leaves artwork unchanged. Full verification: 176 tests, Ruff and mypy clean. Corrected a false-positive process check that had matched its own shell command, confirmed Krita was closed using its exact process name, and safely deployed the current nine-file plugin.

## S330 — 2026-08-07 (Isolated Krita review-engine protocol)

Advanced Cel-Shaded-Generator A2 issue #10 (`9b00a80`) with the explicit AppImage-to-engine boundary promised by A1. The standalone `cel-shaded-generator-engine` accepts one bounded, versioned JSON review request over standard streams; the dependency-free Krita client uses no shell and enforces executable discovery, a five-second timeout, bounded input/output, matching protocol and request identifiers, structured errors, and an operation allowlist. Malformed input produces no traceback leakage. The full suite is now 172 passing tests with clean Ruff and mypy. Landmark UI and user-facing engine-path configuration remain.

## S318 — 2026-08-06 (Cel-Shaded-Generator native crash containment complete)

Completed Cel-Shaded-Generator issue #6 (`ae01b14`): built-in ordinary/screentone/reference/incremental colorization and temporal/graph-refined animation Qt workers now execute in fresh spawned processes, while interactive ARAP uses the restartable persistent worker from S317. ## S317 — 2026-08-06 (Persistent isolated ARAP interaction)

Cel-Shaded-Generator added a lazily started, restartable persistent worker and routed Qt ARAP mesh dragging through it (`0dd8381`, `fbaa525`). Healthy requests reuse the process; crash or timeout discards it and the next request starts a replacement. The anonymized CPU baseline measured ~311 ms cold startup, then 8.78 ms isolated versus 8.75 ms direct (~0.04 ms steady-state overhead), validating persistence for interactive dragging while retaining fresh workers for batch work. Core coverage is 129 passing tests and GUI coverage remains 103; batch Qt routing keeps issue #6 In Progress.

## S316 — 2026-08-06 (Cel-Shaded-Generator native-worker containment core)

Started Cel-Shaded-Generator Phase 0 crash containment (`19e7cf3`, `ce6d9cf`, `d405c7f`, `5077e57`, `57964e7`): a stable GUI-independent request contract now executes native-heavy jobs in fresh spawned processes with hard-crash/EOF detection, termination on adaptive user-capped timeout or cancellation, and automatic recovery on the next request. Optional local JSONL diagnostics are allowlisted to operation, array dimensions, timing, outcome, and caught stack trace, excluding artwork pixels and request filenames. Timeout/cancellation cleanup escalates from SIGTERM to a bounded forced kill so hostile native code cannot hang the host. Diagnostics default to the XDG state directory with seven-day or 20 MiB rotation and a five-minute timeout cap. Seven containment tests bring the core suite to 125 passing; GUI routing and process-overhead measurement remain, so issue #6 stays In Progress.

## S308 — 2026-08-05 (Manga: ARAP rigging UI + real-time GUI wiring, closing issue #194)

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): completed MCA.12, closing issue #194 fully — the roadmap's own "highest-effort item" (S307 shipped the algorithmic core; this session wires it into a real rigging UI).

**`gui/src/elements/manga/mesh_overlay_editor.py::MeshOverlayEditor` — rigging UI.** A `QGraphicsView`, architecturally parallel to `MangaCanvasEditor`: load an image, paint a binary mask over the region to puppeteer (reusing the same freehand-paint interaction as the scribble layer, at a coarser default pen width), generate a mesh over it via `generate_mesh()`, and drag any mesh vertex to pose it. Every mouse-move during a drag re-solves `arap_deform()` from the *original rest pose* against the full accumulated set of dragged/pinned anchors (not incrementally from the previous frame — `arap_deform()` has no incremental mode, and mixing the two would double-count deformation), giving the "real-time re-solve as vertices move" behavior issue #194 asked for. A custom `_MeshOverlayItem(QGraphicsItem)` draws the live triangle wireframe + vertex dots (anchored vertices highlighted red) directly from the current vertex array on each `update()`, rather than one `QGraphicsItem` per vertex/edge.

**Deliberately synchronous, not the usual `QThread`-worker pattern.** Every other manga solver in this codebase (`ColorizeWorker`, `AnimationColorizeWorker`, etc.) dispatches its compute to a background `QThread` to avoid blocking the UI. `arap_deform()` during a drag deliberately does **not** follow that pattern: empirical timing showed ~17ms mean solve time for a ~64-vertex mesh, and an async dispatch would let the displayed pose visibly lag behind the mouse mid-drag — the opposite of what "real-time re-solve" should feel like. Solving synchronously on the GUI thread is the pragmatic choice at this mesh scale; documented as a known scaling limit for larger meshes if that ever becomes a problem.

**New "Manga Puppeteering" tab** (`gui/src/tabs/manga/puppeteering_tab.py::MangaPuppeteeringTab`) — a third "Manga" category tab alongside Colorization and Animation, wired into `gui/src/windows/main/_tab_registry.py` the same way the other two are. Load Image / Paint Mask (with pen-width control + Clear Mask) / Grid Step spinbox + Generate Mesh / Reset Pose, mirroring the Animation Tab's own "test/exercise harness for the already-built solver" posture — no skeleton/bone hierarchy UI, no keyframe timeline, no per-triangle pixel image warping (the mesh wireframe overlays the static image; actually deforming the *pixels* per triangle is a real, separate follow-up, not attempted here).

**Real intermittent native-crash hazard found and mitigated during development, documented transparently.** `arap.py` is the first module in `backend/src/manga/` to call `np.linalg.svd` (every other solver only uses `scipy.sparse.linalg.splu`). Running the new test suite's SVD-heavy cases under the full `backend/test/` pytest collection (which loads ~140 native extension modules in-process, including PySide6/Shiboken, torch, av, jpype, maxflow) intermittently (~40% of runs, measured across 5+ repeated invocations) crashed with a `SIGSEGV` inside `numpy.linalg.svd`'s C implementation, or occasionally a bizarre downstream `SystemError`/`TypeError` in unrelated stdlib code (`re`'s parser, `defaultdict.__reduce_ex__`) — classic symptoms of native heap corruption surfacing unpredictably after the fact, not an algorithmic bug (the ARAP math's own correctness was independently, repeatedly verified whenever the process *did* survive to completion). Root-caused via elimination, not guessed: (1) `np.linalg.svd` in a tight loop never crashed standalone, even with PySide6 and every heavy library `conftest.py` imports already loaded; (2) 5 repeated runs of the other 95 pre-existing manga tests (zero `np.linalg.svd` calls between them) never crashed; (3) it only crashed when this module's SVD-heavy tests ran as part of the *full* pytest collection, and only with enough cumulative SVD call volume (isolating either heavy test alone, repeated 3x each, never crashed either — it needed the full file's combined volume). Mitigated two ways: (a) added the same `telemetry.MANGA_COLORIZE_LOCK` serialization every other manga solver already uses, to `arap_deform()` and `generate_mesh()`'s `Delaunay()` call (also native/compiled); (b) reduced the two heaviest tests' SVD call volume (coarser test meshes, fewer iterations) to a level that still validates the exact same correctness properties. 15/15 clean runs after both changes (10 standalone + 5 full-suite), versus the ~40% failure rate before. The underlying native co-loading fragility is **not fully root-caused or eliminated** — flagged plainly as a genuine residual risk for any future SVD-heavy manga code, not something this session could responsibly claim to have "fixed."

**Verification**: `gui/test/manga/test_mesh_overlay_editor.py` (18 new — mask painting/clearing, mesh generation shape/signal/anchor-reset, drag-pins-anchor-at-target, drag-solves-from-rest-not-previous-pose, pose-changed signal, drag-target-canvas-clipping, reset-pose clears anchors/restores rest + emits signal + no-ops before a mesh exists, nearest-vertex pick-radius hit-testing), `gui/test/manga/test_puppeteering_tab.py` (12 new — image loading, paint-mode/pen-width/clear-mask wiring, generate-mesh guard rails (no image, no mask, too-few-grid-points) and success path (including auto-switching off paint mode), reset-pose button, pose-changed status updates). 109 backend manga tests, 93 GUI manga tests total, all passing. `ruff check --fix` clean; `python -m compileall` clean on `backend/src`/`gui/src`. Manually verified end-to-end outside pytest: load → paint mask → generate mesh → drag a vertex to a target → reset pose, all working correctly with real Qt event handling.

**Roadmap status**: the entire deterministic mathematical-optimization track (§1–§6) is now fully shipped for Manga Colorization & Animation. Remaining open manga items: #189 (graph-QP fallback colorizer, still correctly gated by the roadmap's own "only build if OT proves insufficient" condition — no such evidence exists), and the `[Research]`-tagged diffusion/DPO items (§2.5, §3.4, §4/#198/#199/#200), which need a ComfyUI spike rather than direct implementation.

## S307 — 2026-08-05 (Manga: ARAP mesh puppeteering algorithmic core, partial — issue #194)

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): shipped MCA.12 partial, progressing issue #194 — the roadmap's own "highest-effort item" (~2+ weeks: mesh generation, rigging UI, real-time re-solve). This session ships the deterministic algorithmic core; rigging UI and real-time GUI wiring are a real, separately-scoped follow-up (kept issue open — see below).

**`backend/src/manga/arap.py` — `generate_mesh()` + `arap_deform()`.** `generate_mesh(mask, grid_step)` samples a regular grid of candidate points inside a caller-supplied binary mask, Delaunay-triangulates them, discards any triangle whose centroid falls outside the mask (so the mesh doesn't bridge across concave gaps via the triangulation's convex-hull tendency), and drops/reindexes any vertex left unreferenced by a surviving triangle — an unreferenced vertex would otherwise leave a structurally zero row in the solver's Laplacian, which `splu` can't factor. `arap_deform(vertices, triangles, anchors, n_iters)` alternates: (1) a **local step** — per-triangle optimal rotation via the classic 2D orthogonal Procrustes solution (SVD of a 2x2 edge-covariance matrix, with the standard reflection-correction when the raw solution is a mirror rather than a rotation); (2) a **global step** — solving `L q = b` where `L` is the combinatorial graph Laplacian over the mesh's edges (each triangle occurrence of an edge contributes weight 1) and `b` is built from the current per-triangle rotations, with anchor vertices pinned via the exact same Dirichlet row-replacement trick `colorization.py`'s `_solve_chrominance` already uses for scribbled pixels. `L` itself doesn't depend on the rotations (only mesh topology + rest positions), so it's factorized once via `splu` and the factorization is reused across all `n_iters` alternations — the same "build once, reuse across iterations" optimization pattern this session's other iterative solvers (Sinkhorn, graph-cut) already lean on where applicable.

**Verified correct, not just plausible-sounding.** The real ARAP correctness property — reproducing rigid motion exactly when nothing contradicts it — was directly tested: with every boundary vertex of a mesh anchored to a rigidly-rotated (20°) target position, the *free* interior vertices converge to their own analytically-rotated positions to well under 1% of the mesh's own scale (mean error ~0.0018px against a ~127px mesh diagonal in one manual verification run, and asserted at a looser but still meaningful <1%-of-scale bound in the automated test to stay robust across mesh/RNG variation). A single moved anchor with the rest of the mesh free produces a finite, smoothly-decaying local deformation (no blow-up/NaN) — displacement negatively correlates with distance from the anchor, exactly the "locally rigid, globally flexible" behavior ARAP is supposed to have. Solve time: ~33ms for a single-anchor update on a ~100-vertex mesh.

**Deviations from the roadmap's plan, documented transparently:** (1) pure Python/NumPy/SciPy, not the originally-proposed C++ `base::manga::arap_deform()` Eigen kernel — the same deliberate, already-established deviation as every other solver shipped this milestone (`colorization.py`, `temporal.py`, `optimal_transport.py`, `graph_cut.py`, `quadtree.py`), for the same reason: avoids a new build-system surface before the algorithm is proven correct, and SciPy's sparse LU + NumPy's SVD are fast enough at interactive scale. (2) No dependency on issue #184 (line art extraction for automatic character isolation, itself unbuilt) — `generate_mesh()` accepts any caller-supplied binary mask, matching the roadmap's own already-established "manual-mask MVP first" pattern (§1.1). (3) The formulation's "skeleton-length constraint for anatomical regularity" is not implemented — an optional refinement on top of the core energy (pinning specific bone-like edge lengths), not required for the base algorithm's correctness, and there's no rigging/skeleton UI yet to define which edges would even be constrained.

**Verification**: `backend/test/manga/test_arap.py` (14 new — mesh-generation shape/index-range/vertex-coverage/grid-density/too-few-points/bounds correctness, identity-anchor no-op, output-shape, exact-anchor-pinning, the full-boundary rigid-rotation reproduction test described above, single-anchor finite-and-decaying-displacement, no-anchors/out-of-range-anchor validation, and a non-divergence check across iteration counts). 109 backend manga tests total, all passing. `ruff check --fix` clean; `python -m compileall` clean on `backend/src`.

**Roadmap status**: issue #194 stays open, tracking the remaining rigging-UI + real-time-GUI-wiring scope — the same two-part delivery pattern issue #191 (quadtree acceleration) already followed this milestone. Fully-open manga items now: #194 (this issue, remaining scope), #189 (graph-QP fallback colorizer, still correctly gated by the roadmap's own "only build if OT proves insufficient" condition), and the `[Research]`-tagged diffusion/DPO items (§2.5, §3.4, §4/#198/#199/#200).

## S306 — 2026-08-05 (Manga: live-stroke incremental resolve GUI wiring, closing issue #191)

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): completed MCA.9, closing issue #191 fully (S305 shipped the backend mechanism; this session wires it into the GUI).

**`MangaCanvasEditor` (§5.1) — stroke bounding-box tracking.** `_accumulate_stroke_bbox()` expands a running pixel-space bbox as the user paints (called from `mousePressEvent`/`mouseMoveEvent`), padded by half the pen width (a stroke's visible extent reaches beyond its path centerline) and clipped to canvas bounds; `mouseReleaseEvent` finalizes it into `get_last_stroke_bbox()`, reset to `None` by `clear_scribbles()` and `set_line_art()`. This is the missing piece `colorize_region_incremental()` (S305) needed to know *which* region actually changed.

**Manga Colorization Tab (§6.1) — "Live Preview" checkbox.** Enabled for the scribble-based modes only (Scribble, Screentone) — the reference mode's two-image `colorize_reference` signature doesn't fit `colorize_region_incremental`'s single-image contract, so selecting it disables and unchecks the box, mirroring the existing "Load Reference…" button's mode-gating pattern. Connected to `MangaCanvasEditor.scribble_changed`: the first stroke after enabling (or on freshly loaded line art) triggers one ordinary full solve to seed a baseline (`colorize_region_incremental` has no "solve from nothing" path); every stroke after that dispatches a new `IncrementalColorizeWorker` (`gui/src/helpers/manga/colorize_worker.py`, `QThread` subclass, same pattern as `ColorizeWorker`) that re-solves only the touched quadtree window and composites it into the running baseline. Quadtree leaves are computed once per loaded image and cached (`self._quadtree_leaves`) rather than rebuilt per stroke, since the partition only depends on the static line art, not the scribbles.

**Deliberate design choice, documented transparently:** a stroke completing while a previous solve is still in flight is silently skipped, not queued — the scribble bitmap already has the new stroke painted onto it by the time the next solve runs, so nothing is lost, just delayed by one stroke; a reasonable best-effort trade-off for a HITL live preview, not a correctness gap.

**Manually verified end-to-end with real threads and real cv2**, outside pytest (per issue #196's own documented finding, `gui/test/conftest.py`'s global `cv2` mock plus a real background `QThread` running the real solver is a crash hazard, so GUI tests mock the worker/backend calls — see below): loaded a synthetic line-art image, painted a seed stroke, toggled Live Preview on (triggering and completing a full solve via genuine cross-thread signal delivery -- `QThread.wait()` followed by `app.processEvents()` to flush the queued connection, not a synchronous call), painted a second stroke elsewhere, confirmed an `IncrementalColorizeWorker` was dispatched and its result correctly composited into the running baseline.

**Verification**: `gui/test/manga/test_canvas_editor.py` (+6: stroke-bbox padding/expansion, canvas-bounds clipping, finalization-on-release via a minimal fake-mouse-event stub, reset-on-clear, reset-on-new-line-art), `gui/test/manga/test_colorization_tab.py` (+13: mode-gating of the checkbox, no-op when unchecked, full-solve-seeds-baseline, incremental-worker-dispatch-with-baseline, skip-when-worker-busy, no-op-without-dirty-bbox, baseline-seeded-by-any-full-solve-finish, canvas/status update on incremental completion, baseline reset on new line art load). 95 backend manga tests (unaffected), 63 GUI manga tests total (up from 46), all passing. `ruff check --fix` clean; `python -m compileall` clean on `backend/src`/`gui/src`.

**Roadmap status**: fully-open manga items remaining: #189 (graph-QP fallback colorizer, correctly gated by the roadmap's own "only build if OT proves insufficient" condition), #194 (ARAP mesh puppeteering, the highest-effort roadmap item), and the `[Research]`-tagged diffusion/DPO items (§2.5, §3.4, §4/#198/#199/#200).

## S305 — 2026-08-05 (Manga: quadtree-accelerated dirty-region resolve, partial — issue #191)

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): shipped MCA.9 partial, progressing issue #191 (kept open — see below).

**`backend/src/manga/quadtree.py` — spatial partitioning + windowed incremental resolve.** `build_quadtree(gray, max_depth=6, min_size=32, variance_threshold=150.0)` recursively subdivides a grayscale image into flat-vs-detailed leaf regions: a region splits into 4 quadrants when its local intensity variance exceeds the threshold (a proxy for line art/screentone detail) and it's still larger than `min_size` and shallower than `max_depth`; otherwise it stays a single leaf. This produces exactly the "flat vs. detailed regions" split the roadmap calls for — detailed regions end up partitioned into many small leaves, flat regions into a few large ones. `colorize_region_incremental(gray, scribble_rgb, scribble_mask, prev_result, dirty_bbox, ...)` takes a "dirty" bounding box (the latest stroke's extent), expands it to the union of whichever quadtree leaves it touches plus a halo, re-solves only that window through any existing §2.1/§2.2/§2.4 colorizer (`colorize_fn`, default `colorize_scribble`), and composites the windowed result into `prev_result` — everything outside the window is copied through unchanged.

**Verified real speedup, not just a plausible-sounding design.** A stroke landing in a finely-partitioned detailed region re-solves a ~56x56px window in ~0.01s, versus ~3.5s for a full 800x800-page solve — roughly 300x faster for that case. A stroke landing in a large flat region correctly re-solves a proportionally larger window (flat regions get large leaves since there's no fine detail to preserve, so a wide area is one contiguous solve unit) — a real, intentional asymmetry, not a bug: uniform regions have nothing forcing finer partitioning.

**Marked "🔄 Partial," not "✅ Done" — why, stated plainly.** This ships the partitioning/windowed-resolve *mechanism*, not a live per-stroke GUI dispatch loop. The Manga Colorization Tab (§6.1) and Manga Animation Tab (§6.2) both still solve on an explicit "Colorize"/"Colorize Sequence" button click, not per-stroke. Issue #191's own text explicitly allows this: "Deferrable for an initial 'solve on demand' (non-live) MVP" — which is this project's actual current state. Wiring `colorize_region_incremental()` into a real live-stroke dispatch loop (debounced re-solve on `MangaCanvasEditor.scribble_changed`) is tracked as a real, separately-scoped follow-up, not attempted here.

**Real pre-existing solver limitation found during development, documented not fixed.** An early test fixture using a dense high-contrast striped synthetic pattern (every 4th column dark) reproducibly triggered `RuntimeError: Factor is exactly singular` inside `colorize_scribble`'s SuperLU factorization for some sub-window crops of that pattern — confirmed via direct reproduction, not a guess, and confirmed pre-existing (the same crop fails identically calling `colorize_scribble` directly, with zero `quadtree.py` code involved). A real numerical edge case in `build_levin_system`'s Levin-weight construction for that specific pathological repeating structure. Not fixed here — `colorization.py` is out of scope for this issue — worked around in this module's own tests by switching to a sparse-dot screentone pattern (matching `test_screentone.py`'s already-established `_screentone_gray` fixture), which doesn't trigger it. Worth a dedicated follow-up investigation if a real scribbled image ever hits the same structure.

**Verification**: `backend/test/manga/test_quadtree.py` (13 new: quadtree leaf-coverage/no-overlap correctness, uniform-image single-leaf, detailed-vs-flat subdivision comparison, max-depth/min-size bounds, incremental-resolve shape/dtype, outside-window-pixels-unchanged, detailed-region-small-window verification, no-scribbles-in-window/dirty-bbox-outside-all-leaves no-op cases, shape-mismatch validation, custom `colorize_fn` pass-through, and a timing assertion that incremental beats full-page). 95 backend manga tests total, all passing. `ruff check --fix` clean; `python -m compileall` clean on `backend/src`.

**Roadmap status**: issue #191 stays open, tracking the remaining live-GUI-wiring scope. Fully-open manga items now: #189 (graph-QP fallback colorizer — the roadmap's own text gates this on "only build if #188 proves insufficient," and no such evidence exists yet, so it's correctly left unbuilt), #194 (ARAP mesh puppeteering, the highest-effort roadmap item), and the `[Research]`-tagged diffusion/DPO items (§2.5, §3.4, §4).

## S304 — 2026-08-05 (Manga: Preference Review Dialog, DPO preference capture — issue #197)

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): shipped MCA.15, closing issue #197.

**`gui/src/components/manga_preference_dialog.py` (`MangaPreferenceDialog`, issue #197) — side-by-side A/B preference capture.** Built early per the roadmap's own rationale ("as a stub capturing preferences to a local SQLite/JSON log even before §4 lands") — the LocalDPO/LoRA alignment pipeline it feeds (§4.1/§4.2) doesn't exist yet, but preference data collection should start as soon as any generative colorization mode ships, and the Manga Colorization Tab already has three working modes to compare (Scribble, Screentone, Reference/Optimal-Transport). The dialog shows two candidate images side by side with "← Prefer A" / "Tie / Skip" / "Prefer B →" buttons; a vote emits `preference_recorded` and calls `log_preference()`.

**`backend/src/manga/preference_log.py` — JSON-lines vote log.** `log_preference(source_a, source_b, winner, metadata=None, log_path=None)` appends one JSON record (`{timestamp, source_a, source_b, winner, metadata}`) to `~/.image-toolkit/manga_preferences.jsonl` by default; `read_preferences()` reads them back, returning `[]` if the log doesn't exist yet rather than raising. **Deviation, documented transparently:** JSON-lines instead of SQLite (the roadmap's own text says "SQLite/JSON," leaving the choice open) — an append-only vote log has no query/update/deletion requirements, so SQLite's transactional guarantees and query engine would be unused machinery; JSONL is human-inspectable, needs no new dependency, and is trivially concatenable if preference logs from multiple installs are ever merged for training. The log path reuses this project's existing `~/.image-toolkit/` local-app-data convention (same directory `gui/src/utils/manager/shortcut_manager.py`'s `keybindings.json` already uses).

**Deliberately not wired into any existing tab.** Issue #197's own scope is "New file: `gui/src/components/manga_preference_dialog.py`" — the dialog + log module, not tab integration. An automatic "Compare Modes" trigger in the Colorization Tab (running two solvers on the same scribbles and opening this dialog with the results) would be a natural, small follow-up, but wasn't built here to stay within scope; `MangaPreferenceDialog(candidate_a, candidate_b, source_a=..., source_b=..., metadata=...)` is directly instantiable by any future caller (a tab, a batch-review script, §4's eventual training loop) without further plumbing.

**Tests**: `backend/test/manga/test_preference_log.py` (9: append/return, parent-directory creation, multi-vote ordering, metadata pass-through/default, invalid-winner validation, missing-file/blank-line read handling), `gui/test/components/test_manga_preference_dialog.py` (7: construction, per-button vote + log-record assertions for all three outcomes, signal emission, metadata pass-through, large-candidate preview scaling). 82 backend manga tests, 55 GUI manga+components tests total, all passing. `ruff check --fix` clean; `python -m compileall` clean on `backend/src`/`gui/src`. Manual smoke test confirmed the log round-trips correctly outside pytest.

**Roadmap status**: §6 (GUI Test Components) is now fully shipped (MCA.13, MCA.14, MCA.15). Remaining manga items: MCA.7 (graph-QP fallback colorizer, explicitly lower-priority per the roadmap's own recommendation), MCA.9 (quadtree acceleration, needs live-interactive-solve architecture), MCA.12 (ARAP mesh puppeteering, the highest-effort roadmap item), and the `[Research]`-tagged diffusion/DPO items (§2.5, §3.4, §4).

## S303 — 2026-08-05 (Manga: Manga Animation Tab, a test/exercise harness for the temporal solvers — issue #196)

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): shipped MCA.14, closing issue #196.

**`gui/src/tabs/manga/animation_tab.py` (`MangaAnimationTab`, issue #196) — GUI harness for §3.1/§3.2's already-built spatio-temporal solvers.** Explicitly scoped as a test/exercise tool per the issue's own title ("test/exercise the animation solvers end-to-end"), not a production animation timeline editor: load a sequence of grayscale line-art frames (multi-select file picker, sorted by filename), scribble colors onto a couple of "key" frames (e.g. frame 0 and the last), run the 3D quadratic-cost temporal propagation solver (`colorize_scribble_sequence()`, MCA.10/issue #192) off the UI thread, optionally follow up with a "Graph-cut refine" toggle running `graph_cut_temporal_refine()` (MCA.11/issue #193) as a second pass, then scrub through and export the colorized sequence as a PNG-frame directory.

**Frame-indexed scribble store over a single shared canvas.** Rather than instantiating one `MangaCanvasEditor` (MCA.8, issue #190) per frame, the tab reuses one editor instance across the whole sequence: switching the "Edit frame" slider commits the outgoing frame's scribble overlay into a small `dict[int, QImage]` and restores the incoming frame's overlay (if any) directly onto the canvas's scribble layer. This reaches into `MangaCanvasEditor`'s private `_scribble_qimage`/`_scribble_item` attributes — the same attributes `gui/test/manga/test_colorization_tab.py` already pokes at directly — rather than adding a new public "swap scribble layer" method to `canvas_editor.py` itself, which is out of scope for this issue (constrained to `gui/src/tabs/manga/`, `gui/src/helpers/manga/`, and `gui/test/manga/`).

**New `gui/src/helpers/manga/animation_worker.py::AnimationColorizeWorker`** (`QThread` subclass overriding `run()`, same JPype-JVM-safe pattern as `ColorizeWorker`): runs `colorize_scribble_sequence()`, then `graph_cut_temporal_refine()` on its output when `refine=True`, emitting the same `finished_ok`/`error` signal pair as the single-image workers.

**Deliberate scope reduction vs. the original §6.2 brainstorm, documented transparently:** no ARAP mesh puppeteering mode and no mesh-vertex control-point overlay — §3.3 (MCA.12, the ARAP backend) doesn't exist yet, so there is nothing for a third mode/overlay to wire up. The mode selector accordingly reduced to a single "Graph-cut refine" checkbox rather than a 3-way backend picker, since only two backends exist and one (graph-cut) is strictly a refinement pass over the other's output, not an alternative from-scratch colorization mode. This is the natural consequence of "test the already-built solvers," not an oversight — re-adding the ARAP mode/overlay once MCA.12 ships is a small, well-contained follow-up (the tab's mode-selector pattern already matches how `MangaColorizationTab` gates not-yet-built modes as disabled placeholders).

**Real environment hazard found and worked around during development, not silently avoided:** `gui/test/conftest.py` globally replaces `sys.modules["cv2"]` with a `MagicMock()` (to keep the GUI test suite from loading the native OpenCV library). Both `colorize_scribble_sequence()` and `graph_cut_temporal_refine()` call `cv2.cvtColor()` internally; running either through that mock from a *real background `QThread`* reproducibly corrupted process memory ("double free or corruption (out)", a hard `SIGABRT`) — confirmed via a minimal standalone repro during this session, not a guess. GUI tests for this tab therefore mock `AnimationColorizeWorker`/the backend solver calls rather than letting a real thread execute them (dispatch-args/button-state coverage only), matching this session's already-established "real cross-thread signal delivery is unreliable inside a test's own control flow" constraint (see `test_colorization_tab.py`'s own docstring) — just applied one step further here, since letting the real solve run at all (not just asserting on its result) is what triggers the corruption. The full load→scribble→solve→preview→export round trip was manually verified end-to-end against the *real* cv2 via a standalone script outside pytest, confirming the tab genuinely exercises both backend solvers correctly.

**Tests**: `gui/test/manga/test_animation_tab.py` (18: construction, frame loading + dimension/count validation, frame-switch scribble commit/restore, scribble clearing, pen controls, colorize dispatch guards, mocked-worker dispatch (args, refine flag, button state), result-finished/error handling, preview scrubbing, export), `gui/test/manga/test_animation_worker.py` (5: constructor arg storage/defaults, mocked dispatch to `colorize_scribble_sequence()`/`graph_cut_temporal_refine()` with/without refine, error-signal emission) — 46 GUI manga tests total (up from 25), all passing. `backend/test/manga/` unaffected (73 passing) — no `backend/src/manga/*` changes, per this issue's constraint (import only). `ruff check --fix` clean on all touched files; `python -m compileall` clean on `backend/src`/`gui/src`. Manual sanity script (outside pytest, real cv2) confirmed the tab constructs, loads a 4-frame sequence, accepts scribbles on two key frames, dispatches the real solver, and updates the preview slider correctly.

**Roadmap status**: §6 (GUI Test Components) now has two of its three items shipped (MCA.13, MCA.14). Remaining: MCA.15 (Preference Review Dialog, DPO capture, §6.3) — independent of any further animation-backend work.

## S302 — 2026-08-05 (Content Gen §1.4 Phase A: ControlNet + IP-Adapter via curated ComfyUI workflows — issue #35)

**Curated ComfyUI workflow JSONs for ControlNet (pose/depth/canny) and IP-Adapter (reference-image) generation, wired into the existing gen-tab ComfyUI launch path.** Matches this section's own Option A recommendation ("route control through ComfyUI workflows — fastest, most flexible"); Option B (native diffusers ControlNet/IP-Adapter in `sd3_wrapper.py`) is tracked separately and untouched here.

**Investigation finding, reported transparently:** `ComfyUIManager` (`backend/src/models/core/comfy_manager.py`) previously only started/stopped the ComfyUI server subprocess and exposed its URL for the user to open in a system browser — there was no in-app mechanism to submit a workflow JSON to ComfyUI's own HTTP API, apply parameter overrides, or supply an extra conditioning image. `ComfyUITab` (`gui/src/tabs/models/gen/comfy_generate_tab.py`) was correspondingly just "Start/Stop server + Open in Browser + log viewer." Per the task's own contingency plan for this scenario, implemented the smallest correct extension rather than a larger refactor.

**`ComfyUIManager` additions**: `load_workflow(name_or_path)` (load a curated template by bare filename under `configs/comfy_workflows/` or by path), `apply_overrides(workflow, overrides)` (generic `{node_id: {input_key: value}}` merge into a deep-copied workflow — the mechanism used for swapping in a ControlNet control image or IP-Adapter reference image without hardcoding per-workflow logic), `upload_image()` (multipart POST to ComfyUI's `/upload/image`, returning the server-side filename a `LoadImage` node references), `queue_workflow()` (POST to `/prompt`, returning `prompt_id`, raising `RuntimeError` with ComfyUI's own error body on rejection).

**Curated templates** (`configs/comfy_workflows/controlnet_generate.json`, `ipadapter_generate.json`) — plain ComfyUI "API format" node graphs, the same format already used by the pre-existing personal reference pipeline at `configs/workflow_api.json` (confirmed `IPAdapterModelLoader`/`IPAdapterAdvanced`/`CLIPVisionLoader` are already a proven-working node combination in this repo). The ControlNet template takes an already-preprocessed control image (pose skeleton/depth map/canny edges) rather than a raw photo, since raw-photo preprocessing needs the `comfyui_controlnet_aux` custom node pack (not part of core ComfyUI, out of scope); the three GUI-facing pose/depth/canny modes share this one template, differing only in ControlNet checkpoint + control image.

**GUI**: new "ControlNet / IP-Adapter Workflow" panel in `ComfyUITab` — mode dropdown (Pose/Depth/Canny/IP-Adapter Reference, each auto-filling its default checkpoint name and image-field label), base/extra checkpoint text fields with tooltips naming the required `ComfyUI/models/{checkpoints,controlnet,ipadapter}/` subfolder (same "user provides, app doesn't download" convention as `configs/parameters.json`), an image file picker, prompt fields, and a "Queue Workflow" button that uploads the image and submits the built workflow on a background thread, logging the result to the existing log panel.

**Constraint respected**: no model weights downloaded or fetched over the network anywhere in this change — checkpoint fields are free text the user fills in themselves, matching how `workflow_api.json`/`parameters.json` already handle checkpoint references.

**Tests**: `backend/test/models/test_comfy_manager.py` (12 tests — template loading, override merging, mocked-urllib upload/queue incl. HTTP-error path) and `gui/test/models/test_comfy_generate_tab.py` (16 tests — mode-switch wiring, `build_workflow()` per mode, queue-handler guard rails and happy/error paths, file-picker browse/cancel). 28 new tests, all passing; full `backend/test/models/` (107) and `gui/test/models/` (43) suites re-run clean, no regressions. Manual smoke test: loaded both templates and applied real overrides through the actual (non-mocked) `ComfyUIManager` path outside pytest, confirming valid ComfyUI API-format graphs.

**Not done** (explicitly out of scope): running generation against a live ComfyUI server with real ControlNet/IP-Adapter weights (none available in this environment, and downloading multi-GB checkpoints was an explicit non-goal); raw-photo-to-control-map preprocessing; Phase B native diffusers wiring into `sd3_wrapper.py`/`SD3GenerateTab` (whose existing `controlnet_ckpt`/`controlnet_cond_image` fields remain unwired stubs, untouched here).

## S301 — 2026-08-05 (Extension §7.15B/D: GIF frame extractor + video clip capture — issues #97/#99)

**Closed issues #97 and #99** — the last two of the four items §53 was split into (§7.15A core capture and §7.14A/B/C shipped earlier this session). Both build entirely on already-shipped extension infrastructure (§7.15A's video-capture helpers, #102's grid-preview-page pattern, `background.ts`'s context-menu conventions) — no new backend/bridge work, matching this pass's frontend-only scope.

**§7.15B — GIF/animation frame extractor** (`extension/src/frames/frames.{html,ts}`): a new "Extract frames…" image context-menu item stashes the resolved image URL under `storage.local.framesData` (same hand-off pattern as #102's `galleryData` — a new tab can't take constructor args) and opens a grid-preview tab. The page fetches the image itself, sniffs GIF/PNG(APNG)/WebP from magic bytes (a hotlinked/CDN `Content-Type` header is not trustworthy), and decodes every frame with the WebCodecs `ImageDecoder` API — a large scrubbable preview with a real Play/Pause (respecting each frame's actual duration) plus a per-frame grid with checkboxes, following `gallery.ts`'s established "detected items → grid → per-item action" shape. Selected/all frames download as a numbered PNG sequence through the existing `download_image` message — no new download machinery needed. **Deviation:** the roadmap's planned `omggif`/`upng` JS-decoder fallback is not bundled. `ImageDecoder` already covers every build target that matters for this feature (chrome/edge/brave all ship it); only the Firefox build lacks it and shows a clear "not supported in this browser" message instead of attempting a fallback decode. This keeps the feature dependency-free, consistent with this session's established bias against adding libraries when a browser-native path covers the target browsers; Firefox parity via a bundled fallback decoder is a contained, separately-scoped follow-up.

**§7.15D — video clip capture → GIF/WebP** (`extension/src/videoClip.ts`, new `extension/src/shared/webpMux.ts`): two new video context-menu items, "Record 5s clip (WebM)" and "Record 5s clip → Animated WebP", both continuously draw the target `<video>` onto an off-DOM canvas via `requestAnimationFrame`. WebM export feeds `canvas.captureStream()` into a `MediaRecorder` — the roadmap's literal MVP plan. WebP export samples the same draw loop at 10fps, encodes each sampled frame independently with `canvas.toBlob("image/webp")` (no browser exposes an *animated*-WebP encoder via any Canvas/WebCodecs API — this is the closest native primitive), and hand-muxes the frames into one real animated WebP file via `webpMux.ts`: a few dozen lines writing the published RIFF/VP8X/ANIM/ANMF container format around each frame's extracted VP8/VP8L(+ALPH) bitstream chunk — container assembly, not an image codec, so it needed no new dependency (unlike a from-scratch GIF encoder, which would need real LZW/palette-quantization work, `gif.js`-sized dependency territory). **Deviation:** true GIF export and the app-side ffmpeg conversion path are not built — the roadmap's own §7.15D text already framed ffmpeg conversion as "optional, app-side," and new backend/bridge work is out of scope for this pass per the issue's own framing. A fixed 5-second clip duration is used instead of a configurable duration/format picker panel, mirroring §7.15A's already-established fixed 5-frame burst rather than inventing a new UI pattern for one feature.

**Verification**: `npm run typecheck` clean for every new/touched file (`background.ts`, `content.ts`, `videoCapture.ts` [now exports `findVideo`/`videoBaseName`/`sleep` for reuse], `shared/messages.ts`, new `frames/frames.{html,ts}`, `videoClip.ts`, `shared/webpMux.ts`, `shared/webcodecs.d.ts` [ambient `ImageDecoder` types — not yet in this toolchain's bundled `lib.dom.d.ts`]). The two pre-existing `shared/api.ts`/`shared/dupTabs.ts` `@types/chrome` overload errors reproduce identically on an unmodified checkout, per prior agents this session. `npm run build:chrome` bundles the new `frames` entry plus the touched `background`/`content` entries cleanly, verified against a locally pinned `typescript@5.6.3` since this checkout's pinned `typescript@7.0.2` still crashes `ts-loader` regardless of this change (confirmed pre-existing by two prior agents this session); `npm ci` restored the pinned toolchain afterward — `package.json`/`package-lock.json` untouched. No test framework is configured for this package (still true as of this session, confirmed by four prior agents) — verification is typecheck + build only, matching #102/#93-95's established precedent. No new npm dependencies were added.

**Not done / explicitly out of scope**: §7.15C (webtoon strip capture → ASP stitch) and §7.15E (video downloader with time-range cutting) both remain unbuilt — both need new app-side/bridge endpoints, out of scope for this frontend-only pass. Frame-extractor Firefox parity (fallback GIF/WebP/APNG decoder) and clip-capture duration/quality config UI are both natural, separately-scoped follow-ups. App-side ffmpeg conversion of WebM clips to true animated GIF remains exactly as optional as the roadmap always framed it.

## S300 — 2026-08-05 (Extension §7.14A/B/C: bg-remove, upscale & auto-tag via the app bridge — issues #93/#94/#95)

**Three thin bridge endpoints wiring already-shipped app ML capabilities behind the extension's context menu**, closing the last three items split off issue #52 into #93/#94/#95. All three follow the existing `extension_api/` bridge pattern (`bridge_handlers.py`-style `{url|data_b64}` payload resolution, `BridgeTokenPermission` + `CorsAPIView` auth/CORS wrapper) rather than inventing new conventions.

**§7.14A/B — background removal + upscale** (`POST /api/extension/cv/bg-remove`, `POST /api/extension/cv/upscale`): both are genuinely long-running (BiRefNet / Real-ESRGAN inference), so — per the roadmap's own "job-id + polling using the app's existing task queue" — they queue Celery jobs via a new `extension_api/tasks.py` (named to match Celery's `app.autodiscover_tasks()` convention, reusing the same queue `tasks/views.py`'s `CoreTaskView` already uses elsewhere, not a second async mechanism) rather than running inference inline in the request. Each view does a cheap synchronous `cv2.imdecode` probe before queuing (400 on garbage input instead of a wasted job), then returns `{job_id, status: "processing"}` (202); `GET /api/extension/cv/status/<job_id>/` wraps Celery's `AsyncResult`. **Upscale deviation, verified before coding:** the anime_6B `RRDBNet` architecture (`esrgan_wrapper.py`) is hardcoded to a native 4x forward pass (two fixed 2x upsample stages) regardless of the wrapper's `scale` constructor argument — passing `scale=2` would just mislabel the same 4x pass and corrupt its own tile-stitching math. `scale=2` is honored instead by always running the real 4x pass and Lanczos-downsampling the result to net 2x. Extension side: `extension/src/shared/bridge.ts` gained `cvBgRemove()`/`cvUpscale()`/`cvJobStatus()`/`pollCvJob()` (**HTTP-only** — a job-id+poll flow doesn't map onto native messaging's single-request/response model without a second design pass; flagged as a deliberate scope line); `background.ts` gained "Remove background"/"Upscale & save" context-menu items (`runBgRemove`/`runUpscale`/`runCvJob`) that show a toolbar badge while polling, then hand the base64 result to the existing `downloadImage()` path — folder-profile resolution, filename templating, and the optional provenance sidecar all apply exactly as they do for any other capture.

**§7.14C — auto-tag on ingest** (`auto_tag: bool` on the existing `POST /api/extension/ingest/`): `bridge_handlers.handle_ingest` now calls `WDTaggerWrapper().tag_with_review()` on the saved file when the flag is set, adding `{tags, review_tags}` to both the response body and the provenance sidecar JSON. **Deviation:** tags are *not* written to any database — §7.7 ingest doesn't do DB writes at all yet (tracked separately as that section's own open "embedding/DB indexing at ingest" item) — so sidecar-only is the honest current ceiling, not a shortfall specific to this issue. Tagging failures are caught and reported (`{"tags": {"error": ...}}`) rather than losing the already-saved image.

**Tests**: 53 `extension_api` tests total (38 pre-existing unmodified + 15 new: `TestCvBgRemove`, `TestCvUpscale`, `TestCvJobStatus`, 3 new `TestIngest.test_auto_tag_*` cases), all passing. Model wrappers are mocked (loading real BiRefNet/Real-ESRGAN/WD14 weights is out of scope for a unit test); Celery tasks run synchronously via `CELERY_TASK_ALWAYS_EAGER` + an in-memory result backend, needing neither a live broker nor a worker — matching how `tasks/tests.py` remains an empty stub in this codebase (no established Celery-test convention existed to extend). New `extension_api/test_settings.py` + `test_urls.py` (test-only) were needed because `manage.py test`'s system-check pass imports the *entire* `ROOT_URLCONF` graph, which includes `tasks.urls` → `tasks/tasks.py`'s pre-existing `from backend.src.database import PgvectorImageDatabase` `ImportError` (an archived DB.6 symbol, unrelated to this change — confirmed via `git stash` that it breaks identically with zero extension edits present). `npm run typecheck` is clean for both touched extension files (`bridge.ts`, `background.ts`); `npm run build:chrome` fails, but confirmed pre-existing via the same stash comparison — a `ts-loader`/`typescript` version mismatch from a fresh `npm install` against no committed lockfile guarantee, not introduced here. Manual end-to-end smoke test (real Django test client + mocked model wrappers, no unit-test scaffolding) confirmed the full happy path: ping feature discovery lists all three new features, bg-remove queue→poll→transparent-PNG round trip, and ingest+auto_tag.

**Not done / explicitly out of scope**: §7.14D (OCR + translation) — a genuinely new app-side capability, correctly excluded from this "wire an existing capability" pass. The §7.11 extractor's "auto-suggest upscale when the best candidate is small" UX hook is not wired (pure extension-side addition on top of the now-working endpoint). No options-page toggle was added for `auto_tag` (the flag exists and works; surfacing it in the UI is a small separate follow-on). Native-messaging transport parity for bg-remove/upscale is not attempted, per the HTTP-only deviation above.

## S299 — 2026-08-05 (Extension §7.9: bulk page grabber grid-preview page, closing issue #102)

**Closed issue #102** — the last remaining scope of §7.9 Bulk Page Grabber (modes A "Download all" and B "Select images…" shipped S208, 2026-07-04): a grid-preview page with size/format/URL filters and per-item dup-check badges. New `extension/src/gallery/gallery.html`/`gallery.ts`, opened via a "🔲 Grid preview…" popup button. The popup asks the active tab's content script for every detected image via a new `collect_page_images` message (`content.ts`; `shared/pageMedia.ts::collectImageDetails()`, a non-downloading sibling of the existing `collectImages()` refactored to share its ≥64px/dedup/URL-scheme filtering while also reporting each image's width/height), stashes the list under `storage.local.galleryData` (a new tab can't receive constructor args), then opens the gallery tab. The page renders a grid with a min-dimension filter, a URL-contains filter, and format chips built from whatever extensions are actually on the page — all applied client-side. Each cell gets its own download button and status badge (idle/downloading/done/error, with the failure message on hover) via a new `download_gallery_item` message; `background.ts::downloadImage()` changed from `Promise<void>` to `Promise<{ok, error}>` to support this while staying backward-compatible with its existing fire-and-forget callers (context menu, `download_batch`, turbo capture). "Download selected" runs the checked/visible items through a small concurrency pool.

**Duplicate-check badges reuse the existing §7.16C client-side pHash pre-check (`shared/clientPhash.ts`) unmodified** — no new dedup logic, per the design note left on §7.16C flagging this exact integration as its natural next step. A "Check duplicates" button runs `computePhashFromUrl()` + `snapshotBestMatch()` per visible item against the already-cached `storage.local` snapshot, rendering "new" / "dup? d=N" / "?" (hash unavailable, e.g. CORS-blocked fetch) badges with the same hint-not-authority framing §7.16C already documented; the existing manual "Refresh snapshot" control in options still owns keeping the cache current.

**Verification**: `npm run typecheck` clean for every new/touched file. Two pre-existing, unrelated `@types/chrome` overload errors in `shared/api.ts`/`shared/dupTabs.ts` reproduce identically on an unmodified checkout (confirmed by stashing this change and rerunning) — a toolchain/typings drift issue, not introduced here. `npm run build:chrome` bundles the new `gallery` entry cleanly; verified against a locally pinned `typescript@5.6.3` because this checkout's pinned `typescript@7.0.2` currently crashes `ts-loader` itself regardless of this change (also reproduced pre-existing). No test framework is configured for this package (no jest/vitest in `package.json`, no `*.test.ts` files anywhere) — verification is typecheck + build only, matching how §7.1–§7.16C were verified. **Not done**: one-click retry for a failed download (the badge surfaces the error but doesn't offer a re-attempt) and an automatic/alarm-based pHash snapshot refresh (already noted as future work on §7.16C) — both small, natural follow-ups, not attempted here to keep this change scoped to what issue #102 asked for.

## S298 — 2026-08-05 (Manga: graph-cut temporal coherence refinement — issue #193)

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): shipped MCA.11, closing issue #193.

**`backend/src/manga/graph_cut.py` (issue #193) — Boykov-Kolmogorov graph-cut temporal coherence.** MCA.10's 3D quadratic-cost propagation solves a whole sequence as one combined sparse system, which works well when local intensity tracking between adjacent frames is reliable, but degrades under fast motion/occlusion — a big appearance jump distorts the intensity-correlation weights the Levin solver leans on, and can leak an implausible chrominance value into an otherwise-clean sequence. `graph_cut_temporal_refine()` adds a second pass: it takes MCA.10's already-solved per-frame chrominance (typically `colorize_scribble_sequence()`'s output) as a *label hypothesis* and uses `PyMaxflow`'s `GraphFloat` (wraps the reference Boykov-Kolmogorov C++ implementation — used directly, not reimplemented, per the roadmap's own recommendation) to decide, per pixel, whether to keep it.

**Deliberate simplification, documented transparently in the module docstring:** the roadmap's MRF formulation is fully general (`E(L) = Σ D_p(L_p) + Σ V_p,q(L_p,L_q)` over an open-ended label set), which would need alpha-expansion for a real multi-label solve — genuine scope creep for a first pass, per issue #193's own stated design latitude. Instead this ships a **2-label binary graph-cut per frame**: label `OWN` (trust this frame's own solved chrominance) vs. label `BLEND` (fall back to the mean of its temporal neighbor frame(s)' chrominance at the same spatial location) — the natural binary reduction of the general formulation for a *coherence-refinement* pass over an already-colorized sequence, as opposed to a from-scratch multi-hypothesis colorization pass. The data term weighs `flicker` (L1 distance between the OWN/BLEND chrominance hypotheses) by `motion` (normalized grayscale intensity difference to the available temporal neighbor(s) — a direct proxy for where MCA.10's intensity-correlation-based tracking is least trustworthy): high motion + high flicker pushes the cut toward `BLEND`; low motion + high flicker keeps `OWN` (the disagreement is real per-frame variation, not a tracking failure). The smoothness term reuses `screentone.py`'s (issue #187) *exact* Gabor-affinity Gaussian kernel (`exp(-||T(p)-T(q)||^2/(2*sigma^2))`, over `gabor.py`'s texture features, issue #185) as a symmetric Potts penalty on the standard 4-connected pixel grid, keeping the OWN/BLEND decision spatially coherent within a texture region instead of flipping pixel-by-pixel. Symmetric-Potts-with-nonnegative-weight pairwise terms are submodular, which is exactly the condition Boykov-Kolmogorov's binary min-cut needs for an *exact* solution — a real, not merely convenient, benefit of the binary reduction.

**Dependency note**: `PyMaxflow` installed cleanly from a prebuilt `manylinux` wheel (`.venv/bin/python -m pip install PyMaxflow`, no C++ compiler invoked), added to `backend/pyproject.toml`'s `dependencies` (alphabetically, same convention as `scikit-image` for issue #188). The task brief's contemplated fallback — hand-rolling a min-cut via `scipy.sparse.csgraph.maximum_flow` — was not needed.

**Verification (manual smoke test)**: ran `colorize_scribble_sequence()` → `graph_cut_temporal_refine()` back-to-back on a synthetic 10-frame 200x200 sequence — the graph-cut refinement pass itself completed in ~0.12s (negligible next to MCA.10's ~37s solve), changing ~0.46% of pixels (the expected behavior for a mostly-consistent sequence: only genuinely divergent pixels get touched). A targeted directional-correctness check confirmed the core claim: with a deliberately implausible ("flickering") per-frame chrominance value, a high-motion neighbor context (>50% normalized intensity difference) pulls the refined output measurably closer to the neighbor-blended value than to the original, while a low-motion context leaves it byte-identical to the original — confirming the data term's motion-weighting actually drives the label decision, not just a fixed bias.

**Verification**: `backend/test/manga/test_graph_cut.py` (19 new: raw-graph node/edge-count and shape-validation tests on `build_temporal_coherence_graph()`, a degenerate-all-zero-cost tie-breaking test, a synthetic cheap-BLEND-pixel selection test, output shape/dtype, the high-motion-favors-BLEND / low-motion-keeps-OWN directional pair described above, an already-agreeing-neighbors no-op test, luminance preservation with the same documented gamut-clipping tolerance `test_colorization.py` already established, input validation, boundary-frame single-neighbor handling, `gabor_kwargs` pass-through, and a 2-frame minimum-length case) — 73 backend manga tests total, all passing. `ruff check --fix` clean on all touched files; `python -m compileall` clean on `backend/src`.

**Roadmap status**: §3 (Spatio-Temporal Animation) now has two of its three items shipped (MCA.10, MCA.11). Remaining: MCA.12 (ARAP mesh puppeteering, the highest-effort roadmap item — mesh generation, rigging UI, real-time re-solve) and the two `[Research]`-tagged diffusion items (§3.4).

## S297 — 2026-08-05 (Manga: 3D temporal propagation for scribble colorization — issue #192)

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): shipped MCA.10, closing issue #192.

**`backend/src/manga/temporal.py` (issue #192) — 3D quadratic-cost temporal propagation.** Extends MCA.4's Levin scribble colorizer so scribbles propagate through time across an animated sequence, not just spatially within a single frame: if an artist scribbles a character's iris in frame 0 and frame 24, the color should interpolate smoothly through frames 1-23. `build_levin_system_3d()`/`colorize_scribble_sequence()` are a direct dimensional generalization of `colorization.py`'s `build_levin_system()`/`colorize_scribble()` — same intensity-correlation weighting, same Dirichlet-constraint scribble pinning — but built over an expanded 3D `(t, y, x)` neighborhood and solved as a single combined sparse system across the whole sequence, so color naturally diffuses along the temporal axis through whichever frames have no scribbles of their own (no separate interpolation post-process). Exactly the roadmap's own recommendation ("same solver, expanded affinity graph").

**Scaling issue found and addressed during implementation**: the 3D system's sparse-LU fill-in scales much worse than the 2D case. A 10-frame 400x400 test sequence exhausted available memory (OOM) at `max_solve_dim=256` — the same proportional default as the single-frame colorizer's 640 — but solved in ~36s using ~4.4GB at `max_solve_dim=128`. `colorize_scribble_sequence()`'s default is therefore 128, with the tradeoff documented in the module docstring: this is scoped for short in-between spans at modest resolution (issue #192's actual stated use case), not full-episode timelines. A chunked/windowed solve is noted as a real, not-yet-built follow-up for larger sequences.

**Verification**: `backend/test/manga/test_temporal.py` (14 new — 3D system shape/zero-row-sum/finite-weight correctness, sequence colorization shape/dtype, monotonic cross-frame color interpolation, unscribbled-middle-frame propagation, input validation, downscale/degenerate-downscale handling, per-frame luminance preservation). 54 backend manga tests total, all passing. `gui/test/manga/` unaffected (25 passing) — this ships the backend solver only; GUI wiring (an animation-sequence-aware tab) is tracked separately under MCA.14/issue #196, not yet built.

**Roadmap status**: §3 (Spatio-Temporal Animation) now has its first shipped item. Remaining: MCA.11 (graph-cut temporal coherence, for fast-motion/occlusion cases where MCA.10's local intensity tracking degrades), MCA.12 (ARAP mesh puppeteering, the highest-effort roadmap item).

## S296 — 2026-08-05 (Manga: Optimal-Transport / Sinkhorn reference colorizer — issue #188)

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): shipped MCA.6, closing issue #188.

**`backend/src/manga/optimal_transport.py` (issue #188) — reference-sheet colorization.** The dominant industrial manga colorization workflow isn't scribble-based — an artist provides one colored reference sheet and an entire chapter of line art is colorized to match it. `colorize_reference(target_gray, reference_rgb, ...)`: over-segments both images into SLIC superpixels (`skimage.segmentation.slic`), describes each superpixel by a structural feature vector (MCA.3's Gabor texture signature + normalized centroid position — matching is done on structure, not raw color, since the target has no color yet), solves for a soft reference→target correspondence via `sinkhorn()` (entropic-regularized Optimal Transport, `min_P <P,C> + eps*H(P)`), and colors each target superpixel as the transport-plan-weighted average of reference superpixel colors. Same target-luminance-preservation contract and `max_solve_dim` downscale-for-solve/upsample-chrominance pattern as MCA.4/MCA.5 (~1-2s solve time for a downscaled page); same `MANGA_COLORIZE_LOCK` serialization.

**Deviations from the original plan, documented transparently in the module docstring:** (1) hand-written ~15-line NumPy Sinkhorn instead of pulling in the `POT` library — avoids a new third-party dependency for something this self-contained; `POT` remains a reasonable follow-up if unbalanced-OT or GPU dispatch is ever needed. (2) Gabor texture + position matching instead of the CLIP-embedding-based matching used by retrieval-augmented pipelines like ColorFlow — stays dependency-light, at the cost of assuming the reference and target share a roughly similar pose/composition (the common case for a character reference sheet applied to consecutive panels of that character). (3) No CUDA/C++ reimplementation — profiling showed SLIC segmentation and per-pixel Gabor extraction dominate solve time, not the Sinkhorn iterations themselves, and both are already bounded by `max_solve_dim`.

**Bug found and fixed during implementation**: `sinkhorn()`'s early-stopping residual check compared `u_new * kv` to `mu` — but `u_new` is defined as `mu / kv`, so that product is `≈ mu` by construction regardless of whether the *other* marginal (`nu`) has actually converged; the loop was breaking after essentially one iteration every time. Fixed by tracking `|v_new - v|` iterate-stability instead, which only shrinks to ~0 once both marginals are genuinely satisfied. Caught by a new marginal-satisfaction test before this shipped, not in production.

**GUI wiring**: `gui/src/tabs/manga/colorization_tab.py`'s "Reference / Optimal-Transport" mode is now enabled with a dedicated "Load Reference…" button (enabled only in that mode, since this workflow needs a second image instead of scribbles). New `ReferenceColorizeWorker` (`gui/src/helpers/manga/colorize_worker.py`) — kept as a separate `QThread` subclass rather than generalizing `ColorizeWorker`, since the reference workflow's two-image, no-scribble-mask signature doesn't fit that worker's existing one cleanly.

**Verification**: `backend/test/manga/test_optimal_transport.py` (13 new: `sinkhorn()` marginal-satisfaction/shape/degenerate-input cases, `colorize_reference()` shape/dtype/luminance-preservation/color-direction/input-validation/downscale cases) — 40 backend manga tests total, all passing. `gui/test/manga/test_colorization_tab.py` (+6 new: mode-enablement, reference-load, reference-worker-dispatch, missing-reference-image guard) — 25 GUI manga tests total, all passing. `ruff check --fix` clean on all touched files; `python -m compileall` clean on `backend/src`/`gui/src`.

**Roadmap status**: MCA-2 phase now down to MCA.7 (graph-QP fallback colorizer, explicitly lower-priority than MCA.6 per the roadmap's own recommendation) and MCA.9 (quadtree acceleration, only meaningful once live/interactive solving is in scope).

## S295 — 2026-08-04 (Load Tab Configuration dialog sizing fix + Manga: screentone-aware colorization — issues #185/#187)

**Meta+S dialog sizing** (user-reported, screenshot attached): `gui/src/windows/main/_load_tab_config.py`'s "Load Tab Configuration" dialog only set `setMinimumWidth(360)`, so `QDialog` auto-sized its height to the layout's size hint — with more than 2-3 saved configs, the `QListWidget` only showed a couple of names before scrolling, and the scrollbar itself was barely visible. Now `dlg.resize(420, 420)` with `dlg.setMinimumSize(360, 300)`, so users can see substantially more of their saved configurations at a glance without needing to resize the window themselves every time.

**Continued Manga Colorization & Animation** (Milestone #6): shipped MCA.3 and MCA.5, closing issues #185 and #187.

**`backend/src/manga/gabor.py` (issue #185) — screentone Gabor feature extraction.** `gabor_feature_bank()`: a Gabor filter bank (`cv2.getGaborKernel` across orientations/frequencies) producing a per-pixel texture signature. **Pure Python/OpenCV, not the roadmap's originally-planned C++ `base` kernel** — same rationale as issue #186's deviation (avoids a new build-system surface before the rest of the HITL loop is proven out end-to-end; a native port remains a valid follow-up). Found and fixed a real correctness gap while validating the feature quality: raw Gabor filter response magnitude is phase-sensitive at the pixel level, so two adjacent pixels *within the same halftone pattern* could still score a large feature-space distance purely from landing on opposite phases of the underlying dot grid — this would have defeated the entire point of using texture features for screentone-boundary detection. Fixed by locally smoothing each filter's response magnitude into a "Gabor energy" map (`cv2.GaussianBlur`, a standard texture-segmentation post-process) before standardizing; verified quantitatively that same-region pixel pairs now score a much smaller distance than pairs straddling a real screentone/flat boundary.

**`backend/src/manga/screentone.py` (issue #187) — screentone-aware scribble colorization.** `colorize_scribble_screentone()`: **not a separate level-set PDE solver** as the roadmap originally specified — a deliberate reuse of #186's already-shipped, already-tested convex quadratic-cost graph-Laplacian solver (`build_texture_affinity_system()`, mirroring `build_levin_system()`'s structure) with pixel affinity weights swapped from intensity correlation to a Gaussian kernel over Gabor texture-feature distance. Both formulations are monotonically-decreasing functions of a local pattern-distance measure — the graph weight is the direct discrete graph-Laplacian analog of the level-set halting function the roadmap specified — so this achieves the identical observable goal (color propagation halts at screentone-pattern boundaries, not raw-intensity boundaries) without a second, separate PDE time-stepping solver and its own stability/reinitialization concerns alongside the one already shipped. Same `max_solve_dim` resolution-cap and Dirichlet-constraint machinery as the Levin solver; comparable solve time (~3.7s for a downscaled 1200x900 image).

**GUI wiring**: `gui/src/helpers/manga/colorize_worker.py`'s `ColorizeWorker` gained a pluggable `colorize_fn` parameter (defaults to the Levin solver for backward compatibility) so the same off-UI-thread worker now runs either colorization mode. `gui/src/tabs/manga/colorization_tab.py`'s mode selector's "Screentone-aware" entry is now enabled and dispatches to the new backend via a `_MODE_BACKENDS` index->function map.

**New defensive lock**: `telemetry.MANGA_COLORIZE_LOCK` serializes both colorizers' OpenCV/scipy-heavy solve path across independent `ColorizeWorker` threads. Prompted by a `RuntimeWarning` ("Mean of empty slice"/"Degrees of freedom <= 0") observed only when two `ColorizeWorker` threads' solves overlapped in wall-clock time during back-to-back GUI tests — never seen with a single solve run in isolation, and every dedicated `backend/test/manga/` correctness test (which never overlaps two solves) passes reliably regardless. This mirrors this project's own already-established `NATIVE_IMAGE_BATCH_LOCK` pattern for the identical risk class (concurrent native/OpenCV-touching calls from independent QThreads); the lock did not fully eliminate the warning under the specific pytest directory-collection-order + threading-timing combination that triggers it (still investigating whether that's a genuinely separate, harder-to-pin-down interaction or just inherent QThread scheduling non-determinism in a fast synthetic test environment), but it's kept as correct, low-cost defensive engineering regardless — manga colorization is a single-shot "click and wait ~4s" operation with its own button disabled mid-solve, so serializing concurrent solves costs nothing in real usage, and every dedicated correctness test (which never overlaps two solves) continues to pass reliably with or without it.

**Verification**: `backend/test/manga/test_gabor.py` (7 new), `test_screentone.py` (10 new) — 29 backend manga tests total, all passing. `gui/test/manga/` updated for the new mode (20 passing, +2 new mode-dispatch tests, -1 outdated mode-count assertion fixed). `gui/test/core/test_load_tab_config.py` (6, unaffected by the sizing change) still green. Confirmed via `git stash` that `backend/test/core/`+`backend/test/database/` produce byte-identical pass/fail/error counts (19 failed, 88 passed, 82 errors — all pre-existing native `base` extension gaps) before and after the `telemetry.py` change.

**Roadmap status**: MCA-1 phase now fully shipped except MCA.1/MCA.2 (text/bubble removal, line art extraction — both need external OCR/PiDiNet model integration, deferred). MCA-2 remains: MCA.6 (Optimal-Transport reference colorizer), MCA.7 (graph-QP fallback), MCA.9 (quadtree acceleration).

## S294 — 2026-08-04 (Manga Colorization: Levin scribble solver, layered canvas editor, first GUI tab — issues #186/#190/#195)

**Started implementing the Manga Colorization & Animation feature area** (`https://github.com/ACFHarbinger/Cel-Shaded-Generator/blob/main/moon/ROADMAP.md`, Milestone #6). This session ships the first working end-to-end slice: MCA.4 (Levin scribble colorizer), MCA.8 (layered canvas editor), and MCA.13 (Manga Colorization Tab) — closing issues #186, #190, and #195.

**`backend/src/manga/colorization.py` (issue #186) — Levin et al. "Colorization Using Optimization."** Scribble-based colorization solved as a sparse linear system: each unscribbled pixel's chrominance is a locally-weighted average of its neighbors, weights derived from luminance correlation over a 3x3 window (research report §5.1). `build_levin_system()` assembles the sparse `(I - W)` affinity matrix via fully-vectorized NumPy (no per-pixel Python loop); `colorize_scribble()` solves it per chrominance channel (Cr, Cb) via `scipy.sparse.linalg.splu`, with the luminance (Y) channel copied through from the input line art untouched. **Deliberately a pure Python/NumPy/SciPy implementation, not the roadmap's originally-proposed C++ `base::manga` Eigen kernel** — SciPy's sparse LU solver is fast enough for interactive single-page use and this avoids opening a new CMake/pybind11 build-system surface just to ship the first working version; a native port remains open as a follow-up if profiling shows it's needed for multi-page batch throughput (noted explicitly on the closed issue). Added a `max_solve_dim` resolution cap (default 640px longest side): above that, chrominance is solved at reduced resolution and upsampled back onto the full-resolution luminance — the same principle chroma subsampling in JPEG/video codecs relies on — keeping solve time roughly constant (~4s) regardless of source image size, with a fallback to full-resolution solving if a scribble is small enough to vanish under the downsample. Found and documented along the way: OpenCV's 8-bit YCrCb↔RGB round trip isn't perfectly invertible when chrominance pushes a pixel out of RGB gamut (intermediate BGR clipping) — a real property of 8-bit color-space conversion, not a solver bug, documented in the docstring and covered by a dedicated regression test.

**`gui/src/elements/manga/canvas_editor.py` (issue #190) — layered HITL canvas.** `MangaCanvasEditor(QGraphicsView)`: three `QGraphicsPixmapItem` layers (generated color at the bottom; line art via a `Multiply`-composition-mode subclass so ink lines always stay crisp over whatever color sits underneath, research report §9.2; a semi-transparent scribble overlay on top). Scribbles are painted directly into a live `QImage` via mouse events and read back as an RGB array + alpha-channel mask for the solver. Found and fixed a real Qt behavior along the way: `QPainter.drawLine()` renders nothing for a zero-length line even with a round-cap pen (no length to cap along), which would have silently dropped single-click dots; fixed by drawing a filled circle instead when the stroke's two endpoints coincide.

**`gui/src/tabs/manga/colorization_tab.py` (issue #195) — Manga Colorization Tab.** Load a line-art image (native-dialog-avoiding `DIALOG_OPTS`, reused `IMAGE_FILE_DIALOG_FILTER`/`load_qimage`), paint scribbles with a color/width picker, run the solver via `gui/src/helpers/manga/colorize_worker.py`'s `ColorizeWorker` (a `QThread` subclass overriding `run()`, not `QObject`+`moveToThread` — this codebase's established JPype-JVM-safe pattern, even though the solve itself is pure NumPy/SciPy/OpenCV with none of the native-Qt-subsystem risk that pattern specifically guards against; still threaded so the ~4s solve doesn't freeze the UI), and export the result to PNG. The mode selector lists all four roadmap colorization modes with only "Scribble (Levin)" enabled — the other three (screentone-aware level-set #187, Optimal-Transport reference #188, graph-QP reference #189) are separate not-yet-implemented backends; leaving the selector in place now avoids reshaping the tab again once they land. Registered as a new "Manga" category (`Colorization` tab) in `gui/src/windows/main/_tab_registry.py`.

**Verification**: `backend/test/manga/test_colorization.py` (12 tests — system-matrix correctness, color propagation direction, luminance preservation with tolerance for the documented gamut-clipping property, input validation, downscaled-vs-full-resolution consistency, tiny-scribble degenerate-downscale fallback); `gui/test/manga/` (18 tests across `test_canvas_editor.py` and `test_colorization_tab.py` — layer/scribble state, pen controls, mode-selector gating, error/info message paths, and the worker-dispatch path). Full `MainWindow` construction smoke-tested with the new tab registered and switched to. Found and worked around a real test-harness limitation, not a product bug: a genuine background `QThread` run inside pytest under this project's `--run-gui`/`q_app` fixture setup does not reliably deliver its queued cross-thread signal back to the test within a bounded time (hangs with a nested `QEventLoop`, silently drops the signal with `QThread.wait()` + `processEvents()`) despite the same worker completing and delivering correctly in under a second when driven directly outside pytest (verified by hand, several times) — the real-thread-dispatch test now asserts on the worker's constructed state instead of racing its signal delivery, with the result-handling logic covered separately and deterministically by calling `_on_colorize_finished`/`_on_colorize_error` directly. Also confirmed (and worth remembering): `backend/test/` and `gui/test/` must not be combined in one `pytest` invocation — `gui/test/conftest.py` globally mocks `sys.modules["cv2"]` for the whole session to keep pure-widget tests lightweight, which silently breaks any backend test needing real OpenCV if collected in the same run; this is pre-existing project test architecture, not something introduced here.

**Roadmap status**: `https://github.com/ACFHarbinger/Cel-Shaded-Generator/blob/main/moon/ROADMAP.md` MCA-1 phase partially shipped (MCA.4, MCA.8, MCA.13); MCA.1–MCA.3 (text/bubble removal, line art extraction, Gabor features) and the rest of MCA-2 onward remain open, tracked on the existing issues (#183–#185, #187–#189, #191–#200).

## S293 — 2026-08-04 (Meta+S: load a saved tab configuration, the missing counterpart to Ctrl+S)
